國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落 ， 仍保持穩健成長 ； 就業重返擴張區間 ，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性 。 惟價格指數續升創高 ， 出口與進口動能轉弱 ，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月增加13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口4,208 億美元，分別月增1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主 。 累計前8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元 ， 較去年同期縮減19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

⚫ 考量法國財政不確定性較高，短期內德國與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。 
⚫ 法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐相對有限下 ， 景氣復甦力道恐仍
較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現分化的情形短期內仍將
持續。 
日 
本 
薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
經濟 ⚫ 8 月薪資年增 3.8%。 ⚫ 9 月消費者信心下降 0.1 點至 35.4。 
短評 ⚫ 雖然通膨預期回升與物價壓力抑制部分消費信心 ， 但薪資與所得成長仍持續支撐家庭支出，使消費者信心維持溫和復甦趨勢。 
中 
國 
歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
經濟 ⚫ 中國 9 月外匯存底較 8 月減少 381 億美元至 3.4 兆美元。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國財政部宣布動用 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務額度，資金將優
先用於「六網」基礎建設等重點項目，加強穩增長措施。 
短評 
⚫ 歐中談判最終達成 16 項共識，依據歐盟發布聲明，中國將自主減少混
合動力車對歐盟的出口量 5 成以上 ， 雙方同意降低部分產品MFN 稅率 ，
預期 2027 年 3 月雙方將再度舉行貿易會談。

世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息

GDP 預估上調至 7.1%。 
⚫ 越南 2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率達 9.95%。

事件 ⚫ 印度央行升息一碼至 5.5%，以應對通膨及匯率壓力。 
短評 
⚫ 世銀認為人工智慧 （AI） 順風仍足以抵銷能源風險，但警告亞洲地區經
濟高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容易受到全球科技支出逆轉的
影響，此外，能源震撼延續到明年，補貼政策恐難以為繼。 
台 
灣 
出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月消費者物價指數年增 2.73%。 ⚫ 9 月出口額為 872.2 億美元。 
重要
事件 
⚫ 美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC)以 337 條款 ， 對特定垂直供電系統及相關
產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達、鴻海及旗下相關企業等遭
列名，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。 
短評 
⚫ 9 月出口續創佳績，反映 AI 相關需求及科技產品拉貨動能強勁，外需
表現維持熱絡；9 月 CPI 創近兩年半新高，主要受天候因素及能源價格
影響，但主計總處預期 10 月將回落。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.12 
一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月修正後928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強 ，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期 ， 帶動企業投資與生產力提升 ；

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗 ， 改口表示 「仍有可能」 採取軍事行動 ，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、 相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場 。 調查對象涵蓋全球約20 家企業 ， 包括台灣的鴻海 、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名 。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術 ，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

則表示，337 調查屬企業間智慧財產權爭議，政府將持續關注案情發展，並於必
要時提供業者相關協助 ； 若最終認定違反337 條款 ， 涉案產品可能面臨輸美限制 ，
對 AI 伺服器供應鏈帶來潛在影響。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修 ， 並且在8 月陸續復工後 ， 帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致 ； 此外 ，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%， 主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動 ， 有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品
及商品價格持續上漲影響 ， 民眾對未來一年通膨的預期升溫 ， 預期通膨率高於2%
的家庭比例升至 79.3%，並使生活水準及耐久財購買意願略為轉弱。不過，穩健
的薪資成長及股市上漲帶來的金融收入，持續支撐家庭所得，有助於緩衝物價上
漲對消費的負面影響，顯示日本內需仍具一定韌性。 
四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來 ， 近期歐中談判達成16 項共識 ， 未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI） 相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將2026 年東亞與太平洋地區 （EAP） 經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應 ， 也更仰賴補貼 ， 此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底 ，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力 ， 運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求 ， 持續性的補貼可能難以為繼 。

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型 ， 經濟前景仍
偏正向。

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC）於10 月 9 日依據 《1930 年關稅法》 第337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

營 運 分 析

南亞科技為全球前四大 DRAM廠商 
南亞科技主要致力 DRAM (動態隨機存取記憶體)的研發、設計、製造與銷售，
開發自主技術，發展智慧財產權，培育關鍵技術研發團隊，落實綠色科技及
智慧製造全面提升製程效能與產能，是全球第4大的 DRAM公司。公司已成
功開發 10 奈米級 DRAM 製程技術，將可微縮至少 3 個世代，並用以發展
DDR5及 LPDDR5 等下世代產品，強化公司自主技術開發與製程轉換佈局。
第二世代 10 奈米級製程技術(1B)產品 16Gb DDR5 已完成設計與試產，於
2024 年量產。南亞科技具備完整 DRAM 產品線，可因應智慧世代的發展，
滿足多元化的產品需求。預期 1C產品於 27Q1 開始驗證。

- 4 - 
個股報告

圖三、DRAM合約價報價走勢圖

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

南亞科完成 787.2 億元私募案，攜手四大客戶搶佔 AI 供應鏈先機 
南亞科日前完成總額新台幣  787.2 億元的私募認購，吸引四大核心客戶參
與。增資後，相關投資方持股比例達 10.19%。雙方同時簽署供應協議，確
保未來能穩定供應多元 DRAM 產品組合。主要目的在於強化南亞科與客戶
的戰略合作，透過 e-SSD 與網通產品等領域，建立 AI 及雲端服務供應商
（CSP）供應鏈中的雙贏局面。私募資金將注資新廠以提升產能，進一步確
立南亞科在 AI 價值鏈中的市場地位。本次私募後，南亞科總發行股數將達 
34.5 億股。

記憶體供不應求，產能持續擴充 
目前產能為 6.5 萬片/月，產能利用率維持滿載，2026Q1~Q3 資本支出 276
億，2026 以不超過 697 億元為上限，設備佔比五成．5A新廠第一階段 35,900
片/月 於 2026~2029 資本支出約 3,466 億．新廠預計於 2027 年初開始裝機，
主要生產 1B/1C/1D製程，預期 27年底開出 20k/月，2028 年達每月投片量
達 30k/月，主要因應網通、eSSD等產品之強勁需求。

AI 帶動記憶體強勁需求，有利報價持續走升 
展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於 DRAM 產能持續滿
載，預期DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產品將持續供不應求。1C產品預期於 27Q1
開始驗證，有利打入 AI 伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此兆豐
預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，毛利率83.3/86.3%，EPS
為 77.85/133.2 元。

記憶體持續供不應
求，報價持續走升。 
2027 新產能主要 用
於生產 1B/1C/1D 製
程。 
大型私募案確保未來
穩定供應多元 DRAM
產品組合。

### Page 1

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Stronger revenue momentum while GM guidance came in softer 
TSMC hosted its 3Q26 analyst meeting on October 15. While we 
view the higher full-year 2026 revenue guidance and the further 
acceleration in capacity buildout as the key positives, the GM 
guidance could be the softer spot. In the meeting, management 
further raised its 2026 revenue growth outlook to grow by around 
44% YoY (from slightly above 40%; USD term), supported by 
continued strong demand especially for AI/HPC. However, both 
3Q26 GM result and 4Q26 GM guidance came in lower than our 
expectations; we believe this is to reflect a steeper N2 ramp and 
overseas fab dilution in the near term. 
AI demand still ahead of supply, with no sign of slowdown 
Key questions among investors largely surrounded demand outlook. 
Overall, we think that management’s tone on AI demand remained 
very bullish. While TSMC did not formally update its long-term AI 
accelerator revenue CAGR, management indicated the CAGR will 
probably be higher, noting demand is extremely robust with no sign 
of slowdown and still far exceeds supply, with multiple drivers 
reinforcing this view, including - 1) agentic AI driving incremental 
CPU demand, which is excluded from TSMC’s AI accelerator 
definition and implies upside beyond the headline CAGR; 2) broader 
adoption of open-source models lowering the barrier to AI usage 
and lifting leading-edge silicon demand; 3) continued demand for 
networking chips alongside AI accelerators; and 4) TSMC’s own 
checks on US data center build progress and power supply, where 
management sees CSPs as very well prepared.  
Capacity build-out accelerating again: N2 capacity more than 
double in 2027; additional N3 fab addition; Texas under evaluation 
On N2, management for the first time guided its N2 capacity will be

### Page 3

more than double in 2027. As for N3, management announced an additional 3nm fab
expansion in Tainan on strong customer demand, with volume production in 1H28. This
comes on top of 1) the ongoing N3 fab expansion in Tainan (ramp in 1H27), 2) Arizona
fab 2 (ramp in 3Q27, slightly ahead of its prior schedule), 3) Japan fab 2 (ramp in 2028),
and 4) continued N5-to-N3 tool conversion. Beyond Arizona, TSMC confirmed it is
evaluating Texas as a potential additional US site, and is in discussion with one customer

building Terafab on utilizing that capacity; while the business model and structure
remains under discussion, TSMC highlighted that its principle is to run the fab by itself.
GM: steeper N2 dilution near term, but “no ceiling” long term
3Q26 GM declined 1.3ppt QoQ to 66.4%, primarily on dilution from the initial N2 ramp,
and 4Q26 GM is guided down a further 40bps to 66.0% at the mid-point on continued
N2 dilution and overseas fabs. Management now expects overseas fab dilution to be

closer to 2% for full-year 2026 (vs. 2-3% previously), helped by larger scale and cost
improvement, but reiterated it will widen to 3-4% in the latter part of the five-year
period as more US fabs ramp. Longer term, management stated there is “no ceiling” for
TSMC’s profitability, though any dramatic increase will not happen overnight. On pricing,
management reiterated a strategic approach by segment, location and technology, with
US production priced differently from Taiwan. Overall, we slightly lower our GM to

66.6%/66.8%/67.7% in 2026-2028E (vs. 67.2%/67.5%/67.8% previously).
CapEx: 2026 unchanged, multi-year step-up reiterated
2026 CapEx guidance was unchanged at US$60-64bn. While management did not
provide numeric guidance beyond 2026, but reiterated that 2026-2028 CapEx will be
significantly higher than the prior three years, and that revenue growth should continue
to outpace CapEx growth. Management acknowledged the tools, material and labor
inflation, but said the majority of CapEx is driven by long-term demand. Net net, we

maintain our 2026E/2027E/2028E CapEx at US$64bn/US$85bn/US$98bn.
High confidence in its structural lead
Addressing questions regarding rising competition, management remains confident in its
technology lead  for both front end wafer and advanced packaging technology. Despite
the later adoption timeframe (planned for in 2030) vs peers on High NA EUV, TSMC does
not see the later adoption than peers as a disadvantage. In advanced packaging, TSMC is
in volume production at 5x reticle size, which management believes no peer can match

today, with 9x and 14x in development and CoPoS for future cost reduction. In addition,
management also highlighted that it expects A14 (volume production in 2028) to be a
bigger node than N2, with customer engagement already exceeding N2’s at the same
stage.

### Page 5

3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance recap

4Q26 guidance: revenue well above expectations, GM below GSe 
For 4Q26, management guided revenue to US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the 
mid-point; based on FX [TWD/USD] of 31.8), GM to 65-67% and OpM to 56.5-58.5%. 
Revenue guidance came in well above GSe of +11.0% QoQ, supported by continued 
strong leading-edge demand including the steep N2 ramp. However, the GM mid-point 
of 66.0% is 1.3ppt below GSe of 67.3% and broadly in line with consensus of 66.1%, as 
continued N2 dilution and overseas fabs are only partly offset by productivity gains and 
cross-node capacity optimization. 
3Q26 results: revenue and EPS beat, GM below GSe 
3Q26 revenue of NT$1,494.2bn (US$46.72bn) was up 17.6% QoQ and 50.9% YoY, above 
the high end of guidance in USD terms and above GSe/consensus by 0.8%/2.1%. EPS of 
NT$29.43 was up 8.0% QoQ and 68.7% YoY, above GSe/consensus by 1.2%/3.7%; 
excluding the NT$2.24 one-off gain from the VIS disposal in 2Q26, EPS grew 18% QoQ. 
GM of 66.4% (-1.3ppt QoQ) landed in the upper half of guidance (65-67%) but 1.1ppt 
below GSe, while OpM of 58.7% beat the high end of guidance (56-58%) on stable OpEx 
at 7.8% of revenue. 
By technology, N2 contributed 8% of wafer revenue (vs. 3% in 2Q26) and N3 33% (vs. 
30%), taking advanced nodes (7nm and below) to 79% (vs. 77%). By platform, HPC rose 
22% QoQ to 68% of revenue (vs. 66%), with smartphone +10% QoQ at 21%. Inventory 
days fell 5 days QoQ to 82 days on higher wafer shipments.

Earnings revisions, valuation and key risks

Forecast changes 
Post its earnings results, we tweak our 2026/2027/2028E EPS by 1.4%/0.0%/0.6% to 
reflect: 1) stronger 4Q26 revenue guidance and the higher 2026 growth outlook, but 
partly offset by 2) lower GM assumptions on steeper N2 ramp and overseas fab dilution.

### Page 7

Reiterate Buy; 12m TP maintain at NT$3,300 
We maintain our 12m TP at NT$3,300  based on a target P/E of 22x (unchanged, 
benchmarked to 1.0stdv above 5-year avg. forward P/E) applied to our 2027E EPS. 
Accordingly, we maintain our 12 month TP for the ADR at US$660, based on an 
unchanged FX conversion of USD/TWD of 30.0 and unchanged ADR premium of 20%. 
Our 22x target P/E multiple is also benchmarked to TSMC’s trading average of 22x 
during its previous revenue upcycle where its 3-year revenue CAGR (2019-2021) was 
28%, vs. our 3-year revenue CAGR at 38% during 2025-2028E. 
Our TP implies 35% (ADR 54%) upside to the last close. As a result, we reiterate our Buy 
ratings for TSMC/ADR. We maintain our constructive view on TSMC’s long-term growth 
outlook as we believe the company’s solid technology leadership and execution better 
positions it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural growth 
opportunities, especially in AI.

### Page 9

Investment Thesis, Price Target Risks and Methodology

Investment Thesis - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
TSMC is a leading global foundry company specializing in cutting-edge nodes. Its leading 
technology stance enables it to enjoy more than 60% revenue share in the global 
foundry market. We like TSMC as we believe its solid technology leadership and 
execution better position it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural 
growth opportunities, particularly in areas such as AI/5G/HPC/EV. In addition, valuation 
looks attractive in our view, with the shares trading at the mid-range of their 10-year 
trading history (P/E: 10-29x). Furthermore, we view TSMC as the key AI enabler among 
our Taiwan Semis coverage, thanks to its leadership stance in leading edge nodes and 
advanced packaging technology – CoWoS (chip on wafer on substrate). We believe TSMC 
will achieve its 25% revenue CAGR target for the next several years, driven primarily by 
increasing silicon content growth and AI/HPC demand, with LT GM to remain at 56%+. 
We are Buy rated.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
Valuation methodology: We have a 12m TP of NT$3,300, which is derived by applying a 
target P/E multiple of 22x to our 2027E EPS. Our 22x target P/E is benchmarked against 
1.0stdv above its 5-year trading average. For the ADR (TSM), we have a 12m TP of 
US$660, based on a USD/TWD rate of 30.0 and an ADR premium of 20%. 
Key downside risks to our views: (1) further deterioration in end-demand recovery 
impacting capacity utilization; (2) slower customer node migrations; (3) slowdown in AI 
investment resulting in lower long-term semiconductor content growth; (4) poor 
yields/execution resulting in worse-than-expected profitability; (5) stronger 
competition resulting in ASP/profitability erosion; and (6) unfavorable FX trend or 
higher-than-expected cost increase weighing on the margin outlook.

### Page 1

M
Idea
TSMC  | Asia Pacific
No slowdown of AI semi
demand; reiterate OW
TSMC is further expanding capacity after considering other
constraints, such as data center build, power and other
components supply.  We think the risk of over-capacity is small,
and stay OW.
TSMC sees robust AI semi demand: Despite CSP power shortages and rising
financing costs, TSMC sees demand far above its capacity. It sees no signs of a
slowdown, which may ease some market concerns. The company did not update its

AI semi CAGR guidance (55-60%), saying it is not meaningful as  the demand is
expanding rapidly.
Comments on  wafer foundry partnership (TeraFab) and competition (Intel): For
overseas fabs, TSMC is evaluating a TeraFab partnership, but TSMC must run the
partner fab. TSMC is also considering other fab campus locations, including Texas.
We asked management about IDM outsourcing from Intel and implications for A14
capacity demand. Management said it would try to be fair to all customers. On

competition, TSMC would expand capacity and “leave no food on the table” for
competitors.
On advanced packaging, we asked whether TSMC would help customers using
Intel EMIB-T advanced packaging: The company is confident about 9x and 14x
reticle size chip support. TSMC’s principle is to help customers ramp products
smoothly, so we take it as no objection if Google/MediaTek needs to adopt CoWoS/
CoPoS in future TPU versions, as we articulated in MediaTek: TSMC CoWoS support

eases MediaTek's 2028 TPU production concerns
We raise 2027/28 capex estimates to factor in 2nm and 1.4nm expansion: The
company maintained its 2026 capex guidance (US$60-64bn) and didn’t provide
next-year capex guidance. That said, it  mentioned more than a doubling of 2nm
capacity build by 2027.  Our initial cross checks with equipment venders suggest
EUV tool bookings double in 2027 (vs. 2026), followed by 25-30% growth in 2028.
We therefore raise our 2027/28 capex assumption to US$83/92bn.

3Q26 results review – margin miss, but revenue upside: Pease see Exhibit 1  and
Exhibit 2  for our earnings review. We lower our 2026 and 2027 margin
assumptions,  offset by higher revenue. We maintain our price target at NT$3,088
and reiterate our OW rating.

TSMC 3Q26 earnings and 4Q26 guidance  review
3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance review – margin miss, but revenue upside: 3Q26
margin of 66.4% misses our forecast of 67.5%, but was largely in-line with consensus,
given the margin dilution of 2nm node and overseas operation dilution.
The 4Q26 margin midpoint of 66% shows continued pressure. TSMC guides for 4Q26
revenue to grow 14% Q/Q at the midpoint on very strong demand, in line with our 10-15%

Q/Q expectation. Full-year revenue guidance is raised to 44% Y/Y, also similar to our
preview.

TSMC: Earnings estimate revisions
Our earnings forecasts decline 3% for 2026,  3% for  2027 and is largely unchanged  for
2028: We lower our 2026/27 earnings forecast mostly due to the margin miss of 3Q26
results and weaker than our initial expectation of 4Q26 margin guidance, and also the
wider overseas fab margin dilution into 2027. We slightly raise the revenue assumption for
2027/2028 as TSMC suggested it would speed up the 3nm/2nm capacity build, but that

also comes with larger depreciation and margin pressure when new production ramps.

Our PT is unchanged at NT$3,088. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027

EPS forecast. TSMC currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of
16.5x since 2018. We find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an
intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.

Our bull and bear case scenario values remain at NT$3,720 and NT$1,700, respectively.

MS Rating
▪ Reﬂecting robust AI capex guidance from

global CSPs, we are OW on TSMC.
▪ Our estimates reﬂect TSMC’s strong
proﬁtability and another year of >40% Y/Y
revenue growth seen for 2026.
▪ Leading-edge (7nm below) demand
remains robust.

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here
NT$3,720.00
24x 2027e EPS
TSMC dominates foundry services: 1)
Breakthrough in EUV tech and materials
accelerates node migration to 3nm and
beyond. 2) Intel outsources its server CPU
production to TSMC sooner than expected.
3) Intel or Samsung exits leading-edge
foundry business. 4) New tech megatrends
such as 6G or AI drive global semi revenue

growth and increase demand for leading
edge nodes. 5) Cost per transistor decreases
further along with node migration.
NT$3,088.00
20x 2027e EPS
Leading position in leading-edge logic
foundry services with some gross margin
expansion: 1) Global semi revenue CAGR is
sustained in the high single digits in 2025-
28. 2) TSMC continues to lead in market
share in 16nm, 7nm, 5nm, 3nm, and 2nm. 3)
It continues to observe robust AI-related
demand. 4) Cost per transistor does not

decrease meaningfully in 3nm and 2nm,
while capex per k is more demanding than
for 7nm and 5nm.
NT$1,700.00
11x 2027e EPS
TSMC's dominance in foundry business
erodes: 1) Global ex-memory semi revenue
growth is weaker than expected in 2025-28.
2) Declining trailing-edge market share
causes revenue shortfall. 3) Intel's
contribution is weaker than expected. Intel
and/or Samsung successfully develop
leading-edge process technology. 4) Intel's
foundry business execution proves

successful and weighs on TSMC with order
competition. 5) Demand for 2nm is less than
expected given high transistor cost for
foundry customers.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

### Page 1

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
CPO/NPO and micro     VCSEL based   optical solutions are the
focus for networking, while HVDC takes center stage for power.
Higher bandwidth and better power efficiency: In the networking space, the
migration into higher bandwidth continues, as players like Accton and Celestica
showcased their 1.6T network switches. We think a bigger theme is the effort to
improve the power efficiency of current mainstream pluggable optical solutions.

First off, one well-discussed approach is the adoption CPO or NPO, and it seems the
latter is gaining more attention due to the technical challenges in the former. Spec
standardization, such as CPX connectors, could further accelerate the proliferation,
in our view. Secondly, copper could still help, as Credo has its 1.6T AEC cables for
inter-tray transmission while many other vendors (Amphenol, TE Connectivity,
Molex, etc.) have their 448G CPC solutions for in-tray transmission. Lastly, the micro

VCSEL based optical modules from Credo and ALAB represent another power
efficient solution (12W/end at 1,6T), which has reliability comparable to copper
(100M hours of MTBF/MTBI) for a transmission distance of 50-100m. We believe
this micro VCSEL based solution could potentially replace the AEC for shorter
distance transmission when copper hits the physical limit in the medium term and
be integrated with the CPO architecture in the longer term. In sum, we believe the

above-mentioned solutions are likely to co-exist for different use cases.
800VDC for power delivery taking the center stage: While the Vera Rubin NVL72
rack already has the in-rack power shelf reference design (110KW/shelf at 3+1
redundancy), Nvidia unveiled another reference design this time, using one 660KW
800VDC power rack to support two Vera Rubin NVL72 racks. Moreover, we also
saw a few ±400VDC reference designs as well. Our conversations with companies

on the exhibition floor indicate that the HVDC adoption should start from late
2026, but likely at a small scale extending into 2027. The next checkpoint will be
Vera Rubin Ultra racks, where increasing power consumption and more hardware
devices for inter-rack scale-up network might take up more pace on the rack, though
the liquid cooled power shelf  should ease the space budget a bit. We believe both
solutions are progressing and will closely monitor the dynamics.

Stock picks: We believe that Bizlink (3665.TW) should be a beneficiary of the
proliferation of the micro VCSEL based optical solution, as it is the assembly partner
for Credo and potentially others as well. Chroma (2360.TW) should see drivers
from power testing, CPU/GPU/TPU SL T/FT, and photonics testing (pluggable/
CPO)tools. Delta( (2308.TW) and Lite-On (2301.TW) should continue to grow with
the increasing power consumption of AI clusters, but more meaningful growth

CPX and NPX all marching forward
Though CPO remains as the holy grail in the space, its yield issue and lower serviceability
still present challenges to the wider adoption in the near term. We believe that the efforts
will continue, but the industry is simultaneously pursuing other more practical solutions

to sort out the power consumption issue of the existing pluggable transceivers,  including
NPO (near-packaged optics), CPC (co-packaged copper) and NPC (near-packaged copper)
Compared to CPO, NPO compromises on the power consumption, but in exchange it eases
the stringent requirement on alignment and serviceability. Given its better technology
readiness and more mature supply chain, we think it might see good traction in the
coming years.

VCSEL based optical solutions gaining more attention
In addition to CPO/NPO/CPC/NPC designs, we see the micro VCSEL based optical

bandwidth per lane is in the roadmap.

In sum, different solutions of data transmission are emerging, trying to improve the power

Power becoming another bottleneck in the AI deployment
HVDC adoption emerging from Vera Rubin
In the power space, HVDC has been one of the key focuses in the AI cluster, as the rapid
increase in AI rack power density is creating growing challenges for conventional power-
distribution architectures. As rack-level power consumption rising from tens of kilowatts
to several hundreds of KWs, electrical losses, cable size, thermal management
requirements, and overall system complexity increase significantly. Under traditional low-

voltage power architectures, delivering larger amounts of power requires proportionally
higher current, which increases resistive losses and places greater stress on cables,
connectors, busbars, and power-conversion equipment.
At the same time, every power-conversion stage introduces efficiency losses and
generates additional heat. As AI deployments scale into multi-megawatt and eventually
gigawatt-level campuses, even small efficiency improvements can translate into

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
meaningful reductions in operating costs, cooling requirements, and infrastructure
investment. Consequently, the industry is increasingly focused on power architectures
that can reduce conversion stages, lower current levels, and improve overall power-
delivery efficiency.
Against this backdrop, high-voltage direct current (HVDC) is emerging as one of the most
important architectural upgrades for next-generation AI infrastructure. By increasing

distribution voltage, HVDC allows the same amount of power to be delivered at a lower
current, reducing conduction losses and improving system efficiency. Higher-voltage
architectures also improve scalability for ultra-high-density rack deployments by
alleviating some of the physical and thermal constraints associated with conventional
power-distribution systems.
Exhibit 18: Better power efficiency for 800VDC architecture
Source: Delta
Exhibit 19: Better efficiency leads to cost savings

Source: Delta
In a joint keynote speech by Delta and Nvidia, the roadmap illustrates the industry's
gradual migration toward higher-voltage power architectures. Earlier AI server generations
primarily utilized conventional power-delivery schemes centered around rack-level 48V
distribution. As rack power requirements increased with Blackwell-generation systems,
industry participants began evaluating higher-voltage architectures capable of supporting

substantially greater power density. Looking ahead, the industry is increasingly converging
around 800VDC as a potential next-generation standard for large-scale AI deployments,
particularly as rack-level power consumption approaches several hundred kilowatts. The
transition reflects the need to improve power-distribution efficiency while supporting the
continued growth of AI computing infrastructure.
Though we also saw the 800VDC power rack reference design for the Vera Rubin rack in a

1-to-2 configuration (one 660KW power rack supporting two Vera Rubin racks), we think
the adoption of this solution might be relatively low, since the Vera Rubin rack also has a
reference design for the in-rack power shelf design

Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 11
Exhibit 20: 800VDC roadmap
Source: Delta
Exhibit 21: 800VDC reference design for VR
Source: Delta
Liquid cooled power shelf on the horizon
The next key check point to watch should be the Vera Rubin Ultra generation, as the
power consumption per rack shall continue to increase and there might be more hardware
devices to enable the inter-rack scale up network, both could take up more space on the
IT rack. That said, the power shelf will shift from air cooling into liquid cooling, which

could narrow the power shelf height by 1U to free up some space as well. Our checks
indicate that both the in-rack power shelf and 800VDC power rack solutions are ongoing
now, and we believe that rack space, power redundancy and system reliability are all the
factors being considered. We'll closely monitor developments on this front.
Exhibit 22: Liquid cooled power shelf
Source: Megmeet

Megmeet, Luxshare, etc.

Exhibit 24: SST vendors on the Nvidia MGX ecosystem board
Source: Morgan Stanley
Looking further ahead, Solid-State Transformers (SSTs) represent a potential next-
generation evolution of power infrastructure. Unlike traditional transformers that operate
primarily as passive devices, SSTs leverage power electronics to provide voltage
conversion, power conditioning, and bidirectional energy management within a single
platform. This enables greater controllability and flexibility while supporting increasingly

digitalized power networks.
Although SSTs remain at an early stage of commercialization and face challenges related
to economics, reliability, and large-scale deployment, the technology aligns well with long-
term data center trends. As AI campuses evolve into utility-scale power consumers
incorporating energy storage, distributed generation, and advanced power management,
SSTs could eventually become a key enabling technology.

### Page 13

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 13
Therefore, while HVDC is expected to drive the next phase of power architecture
upgrades, SSTs represent a longer-term technology roadmap that could further transform
how future data centers distribute and manage electricity.

transformer market, particularly in smaller-power applications. While SST penetration is
expected to remain relatively modest versus the overall transformer market, the
opportunity is significant for relevant players in the longer term.

Gigabyte (2376.TW): AI servers to drive 2H26 growth; Capex 
commitments remain strong; Neutral
20 October 2026 | 10:16PM CST
3-month revenues preview: We expect sequential revenue growth in Oct-Dec on a 
YoY basis, driven by rising AI server racks, leading our 4Q26 revenues YoY to 
accelerate to 79% (vs. 60% / 41% YoY in 1Q26 / 2Q26). However, we expect revenue 
decline in Oct-Dec on a MoM basis which we attribute to AI servers model transition 
and the high base in Sep; nevertheless, we continue to expect revenues sequentially 
increase qoq in 4Q26E. We expect the stronger growth in 2H26 to be driven by 
ongoing AI spending, customers’ faster AI deployment, and Gigabyte’s expanding 
customer base and enhancing backlog visibility. Customers’ infrastructure readiness 
and components procurement should continue to improve in 2H26, supporting the 
procurement on AI servers. The company maintains strong capex commitment, and 
we expect 2H26 capex to stay at a similar high level vs. 1H26, leading 2026 capex to 
grow at 148% YoY to NT$2.9bn to capture the strong AI servers demand, and 
customers’ requirements on diversifying production sites (e.g., Gigabyte is across 
Taiwan, US, Europe, and Southeast Asia).

Earnings revision: We factor in Gigabyte’s Sep revenue and raise our 2026E net

The target P/E of 14.2x is within the company’s historical avg. and avg.-1std forward 
trading P/E. Maintain Neutral.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Gigabyte 
We are Neutral rated on Gigabyte with a 12-month target price of NT$492. Our TP is 
based on a 14.2x 2027E P/E which is based on PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E 
multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s share 
performance. 
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery, 2) faster-/slower-than-expected gaming PC growth, and 3) 
faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

2026.09.11  
HD 현대중공업 (329180)

드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가  Flash Note 
[조선/기계/방산] 변용진 2122-9181 yongjin.byun@imfnsec.com

Check Point  Buy (Maintain) 
HD현대중공업 중형엔진 및 SMR 공장 증설 결정  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건

목표주가(12M) 860,000 원(유지) 
종가(2026.09.10) 453,500 원 
상승여력 89.6 %

HD현대중공업, 중형엔진 및 SMR 시설투자 공시  
기다리던 엔진 증설의 세부 사항이 드디어 공개되었다. HD 현대중공업은 10일 공시를 통해 총 1조 722억원(중형엔진 
8,336억원/SMR 2,386억원) 규모의 시설투자 계획을 밝혔다. 먼저 중형엔진 관련, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 
확대에 대응 목적으로 생산능력 확충을 위한 토지 취득 및 공장 신설 및 생산설비 구축에 28년 5월까지 투자가 진행될 예정이다. 
중형엔진의 생산 능력은 현재 연간 3GW급에서 증설이 완료되면 7.2GW급으로 확장되며, 첫 엔진 출하는 28년 하반기로 예상된다. 
증설되는 신공장의 가동률은 28년 30%로 출발하여 30년 100%로 올라설 전망이다. 이에 따라 연간 1GW당 약 1조원의 매출과 
20%이상의 영업이익(현 엔진사업부 이상) 수준으로 추정되며, 계획대로 증설이 완료되고 일감을 확보한다면 총 4조원 이상의 매출 
증대 효과가 기대된다. SMR 투자 관련해서는 SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주물량 증가에 대응하기 위해 SMR 전용 공장, 설비 
구축 및 기존 공장 최적화에 29년 4월까지 2,386억원이 투입된다. 이는 연간으로 Terrapower의 Natrium SMR 주기기 2기(각 
345MW)를 제작할 수 있는 규모다. HD현대중공업은 이번 증설이 데이터센터용 엔진만을 위한 것이 아님을 강조했다. 데이터센터 뿐 
아니라 국가전력망, 원전용 비상발전기 등 전세계적으로 증가하는 육상용 발전엔진 수요에 대응하기 위함이며, 최근 미국의 육상 
데이터센터 사업이 님비현상 등으로 일부 좌초되고 있음에도 그보다 더 큰 그림에 베팅한 것이다.  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건  
분명한 기업가치 증분의 근거가 되는 증설공시였음에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업의 주가는 발표 이후 하락으로 마감하였다. 올해 
조선업 주가는 YTD 평균 10%이상 하락하며 약세를 보이고 있는 상태다. (HD현대중공업 -10.0%, 삼성중공업 -11.6%, 한화오션 -
25.5%)  
조선사 공히 2분기의 실적 서프라이즈와 함께 올해 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하는 등 우수한 펀더멘털을 유지하고 있으나 이는 
주가 상승재료로 반영되고 있지 않다. 7~8월 반도체발 주식시장 혼란에 따른 전체적 수급 약세도 무시할 수 없는 요인이다. 여기에 
지난해 주가 상승의 주요 동력으로 작용하였던 미국 함정 사업 관련 협력 진전이 느린 점 등이 주가 약세의 주요 원인으로 
분석된다. 백악관 및 美해군에서는 지속적으로 함정 해외 건조의 필요성을 역설하고 있으나 美입법부에서는 지역구 의원의 반대 
등으로 관련 법안의 입법 동력이 약해져 있는 상황이다. 美국회의 이번 회기(119대 회기, ~2027년 1월 3일까지)가 이미 막바지에 
다다르고 있으며 11월 중간선거 일정까지 감안하면 실질적으로 이번 회기중에 관련 법안이 통과 및 발효될 가능성은 미미하기 
때문에, 2026년 남은 하반기에 함정사업 관련 조선업 주가 동력을 기대하기는 힘들어졌다. 다가오고 있는 3분기 실적도 원화 강세 
및 여름휴가/사고 등에 따른 조업일수 하락, 후판 유통가 상승 등 감익 요인이 산적해 있어 2분기에 비해 쉽지 않은 상황이다.  
그러나 여전히 본업인 조선업의 체력은 훌륭하다. 전세계 선박 발주량은 8월 누적기준 5,972만 CGT로 전년동기 대비 +60.6% 많은 
발주가 나오고 있으며 이는 2021년 이후 가장 강한 발주세다. 한국 조선사의 주력 선종인 LNG, 탱커, 컨테이너선의 발주 비중이 
전체 발주량의 67%를 차지하고 있으며 이에 힘입어 조선사들은 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하였다. (상선기준, HD현대중공업 달성 
130.6억 달러/목표 114.7억, 삼성중공업 달성 61억/목표 57억, 한화오션 달성 60억/목표없음) 3분기 실적은 다소 쉬어갈 수 
있으나 여전히 수주잔고에 기반한 추정상 매출/영업이익은 2028년까지 꾸준한 상승이 예상된다. 작년보다 오히려 하락한 주가가 
현저한 저평가일 수밖에 없는 이유다. 조선업 비중확대 및 HD현대중공업 Top Pick 의견을 유지한다.

Document 273: ECB caught in the French drama

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECB caught in the French drama.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

The European Central Bank meeting on 29 October is not
expected to result in an interest rate hike. Instead, the French
crisis is increasingly eroding the need for additional rate hikes
Carsten Brzeski
With the surge in bond yields on the back of the crisis in France, we expect the
ECB to remain on hold at next week's meeting
Back in September, the ECB surprised markets with a more hawkish
message, at least between the lines. With headline inﬂation higher

for longer and core inﬂation expected to increase in 2027, the stage
seemed set for at least one more rate hike at the December
meeting. Some even speculated about a back-to-back increase in
October.
One month later, and after a severe ﬁscal and political crisis in
France, the ECB is unlikely to repeat the hawkish tone at next week’s
meeting. At face value, macro developments since the last meeting
would still justify another rate hike. Headline inﬂation increased

again in September and most leading indicators point to continued
resilience in the eurozone economy. However, the surge in bond
Cookie settings

### Page 2

yields on the back of the French crisis is already imposing a new
restrictive monetary policy stance. In fact, the surge in German
bond yields since the September meeting has already had roughly
the same impact on growth and inﬂation as an additional 25bp
rate hike. For France, it's closer to a 50bp hike. Or in other words, the
bond market has taken over the ECB’s job.
All of this means that the ECB is very likely to stay on hold next
week and will delay any next rate hike decision to December. This is

when new staﬀ projections will be available and when there will
also be more insights into whether higher energy prices will
eventually bring knock-on eﬀects on the rest of the economy or
not. In fact, there are three main parameters that, in our view, will
determine the ECB’s path going forward: i) the length and intensity
of the war in the Middle East. The longer oil prices remain elevated,
the higher the probability of more rate hikes; ii) the knock-on eﬀects

of higher energy prices. So far, there has hardly been any pass-
through of higher energy prices in the rest of the economy. If this
remains the case, the need for further rate hikes drops. If it doesn’t,
the likelihood of new rate hikes goes up. iii) France. Any solution to
the current French ﬁscal woes could bring some relief to bond
markets and allow the ECB to continue hiking rates. Without any
solution, bond markets have taken over the ECB’s role, making rate

hikes less likely (and less needed).
Because it's France
Even if France does not play a role in the ECB’s policy statement
next week, it will be an important element at the press conference.
Political and ﬁscal concerns in France, and the resulting widening in
French spreads, have raised questions about whether the ECB could
intervene through the Transmission Protection Instrument (TPI),
which allows it to purchase securities issued by member states

whose ﬁnancing conditions have deteriorated for reasons not
justiﬁed by country-speciﬁc fundamentals. However, before
activating it, the Governing Council considers a cumulative set of
criteria to determine whether the countries concerned are pursuing
sound and sustainable ﬁscal and macroeconomic policies. In
practice, this includes compliance with the EU ﬁscal framework,

### Page 3

which would, at a minimum, require the proposed French budget to
be fully adopted.
The ECB has nevertheless left itself some discretion, stating that the
intervention criteria “will be dynamically adjusted to the unfolding
risks and conditions to be addressed”. This brings back memories of
the famous “because it’s France” by former European Commission
President Jean-Claude Juncker. Generally speaking, the ECB will be
in a diﬃcult position: intervening too quickly in support of France

may be viewed unfavourably by other member states and
interpreted as monetary ﬁnancing of the debt of a country
unwilling to follow the rules. That argues strongly against early
intervention. Also, don’t forget that the leadership changes at the
ECB over the next months (Schnabel, Lane, Lagarde) could also
aﬀect how the ECB views TPI.
Given the challenges of justifying the activation of TPI at present, we
expect the ECB to adopt a three-pronged approach to address the

French crisis.
1. The ﬁrst line of defence is a more dovish approach to policy
rates. As bond markets are currently taking over the ECB’s job
of inserting a more restrictive monetary stance on the
economy, there is less need for the ECB to hike rates
aggressively, or to hike rates at all. In fact, the ECB has already
started to prepare for this option, as ECB president Christine
Lagarde recently noted that “while growth has been resilient,
since our last meeting long-term interest rates have risen

notably, which will slow growth and reduce pass-through by
more than projected in our September exercise".
2. The second line of defence would be a covert return to bond
purchases. The ECB could instead pause quantitative tightening
temporarily and reinvest maturing bonds in its portfolio
'ﬂexibly', sending a positive signal to bond markets. This had
already happened during the Covid period.
3. Last but not least, should contagion in eurozone bond markets

intensify, we expect the ECB to eventually trigger the TPI. As
long as France remains non-compliant with the European ﬁscal

rules, the ECB might even consider purchasing bonds from
almost all eurozone countries, except for France.

All in all, next week’s ECB meeting looks set to be uneventful on
action but clearly not on words.

Document 290: First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-24).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change
of OCR call
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 October 22 2026 Share
Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter and 3.7% y/y. Core inflation
pressures are elevated and market pricing is primed for a 25bp OCR hike next week, which
we now expect.
Consumers Price Index, September quarter 2026 – key stats

### Page 2

Headline inflation
Quarterly change: +0.7% (prev: +1.5%)
Westpac forecast: +0.7%
RBNZ: +0.8%
Market median: +0.8%, range +0.5% to +1.2%

Annual change: +3.7% (prev: +4.1%)
Westpac forecast: +3.7%, RBNZ: +3.9%, Market: +3.8%

Non-tradables
Quarterly change: +1.0% (prev: +0.6%)
Westpac forecast: +1.3%
Annual change: +3.3% (prev: +3.4%)

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

CPI ex-fuel inflation: +3.0% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
CPI ex-fuel and food: 3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs +2.5% previously)
30% trimmed mean: +3.0% (vs +2.6% previously)
Weighted median: +3.1% (vs +2.7% previously)

Document 297: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

Document 298: France’s unrest adds to an already toxic economic mix

Source PDF: all report pdf/France’s unrest adds to an already toxic economic mix.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Décembre 2018 53
Conjoncture française
Impact macroéconomique du mouvement des gilets jaunes :
une estimation difficile à ce stade
Estimer l’impact économique du mouvement des
gilets jaunes est un exercice difficile et incertain à la
date de finalisation de cetteNote de conjoncture (13
décembre 2018). Ce mouvement, qui a pris une
forme assez inédite, a en effet commencé à la
mi–novembre, soit relativement récemment, et se
poursuit encore au moment où s’achève la rédaction

de cette Note. Très peu d’indicateurs
macroéconomiques sont donc connus à ce stade.
Centré au départ sur la hausse des prix du carburant
automobile, le mouvement des gilets jaunes s’est
ensuite élargi à d’autres revendications, notamment
sur le pouvoir d’achat. Il a surtout pris la forme de
blocages routiers, mais aussi de manifestations
parfois émaillées de violence, à Paris comme en
province et outre–mer.
Si ce mouvement ne comporte pas de précédent

historique directement comparable, on peut tout de
même tenter de lister les canaux par lesquels il est
susceptible d’affecter l’activité économique.
Un impact probable sur la consommation,
notamment de services
Du côté de la production industrielle, les blocages de
routes et de dépôts pétroliers ont pu engendrer des
retards de livraison et cert aines entreprises peuvent
pâtir de problèmes d’approvisionnement.
Néanmoins, ces blocages semblent à ce stade d’une

ampleur moindre que ceux qui avaient affecté
l’activité économique pendant, par exemple, les
grèves massives de décembre 1995, lesquelles
avaient ôté environ 0,2 point à la croissance
trimestrielle du PIB.
En revanche, le mouvement des gilets jaunes est de
nature à pénaliser la consommation, localement
assez durement, et par plusieurs canaux. Tout
d’abord, les blocages, notamment routiers, ont pu
conduire les ménages à reporter ou annuler certaines

dépenses. Les dépenses en biens peuvent certes être
en partie reportées. Mais cet effet de report est en
général moindre pour les dépenses en services (en
particulier dans l’hébergement–restauration, les loisirs,
les transports) qui pourraient donc connaître des pertes
sèches plus marquées.
Un effet sur le tourisme
Car, aux effets directs des blocages, s’ajoute le
caractère anxiogène des s cènes de violence qui ont
marqué le mouvement, en particulier les samedis 24

novembre et, plus encore, 1er et 8 décembre. Certaines
se sont déroulées dans des lieux symboliques du pays et
hautement touristiques (avenue des Champs–Elysées et
Arc de Triomphe à Paris). On peut donc s’attendre à ce
qu’elles pénalisent les activités liées au tourisme.
La France a déjà connu des épisodes émeutiers, par
exemple en novembre 2005. Les données disponibles
suggèrent néanmoins que ces événements n’ont sans
doute pas eu d’impact significatif sur l’activité

touristique. Celles-ci concernaient alors la région
parisienne, mais pas le cœur touristique de la capitale.
À l’inverse, les attentats de novembre 2015 à Paris,
évènements de nature certes très différente, ont pesé
pendant plusieurs mois sur le tourisme, ôtant
0,2 point de PIB à la croissance annuelle de 2016 (cf.
Insee, 2016 et Beatriz, 2018).
À titre illustratif, la chronique des glissements annuels
des nuitées dans les hôtels figure sur le graphique

ci–dessous pour la période 2015–2016. La
fréquentation touristique dans l’hôtellerie a nettement
baissé après les attentats de novembre 2015
(graphique ). C’est – logiquement – la clientèle
étrangère qui a été la plus touchée. L ’attentat du 14
juillet 2016 à Nice a sans doute eu un effet du même
type, même s’il a été moins marqué. Au moins deux
autres évènements ont vraisemblablement eu aussi un
impact sur cette série durant la période considérée : les

manifestations du printemps 2016, dont la violence
ponctuelle n’était néanmoins pas comparable à celle du
1er décembre 2018, et, à l’inverse, l’Euro de football
organisé en France en juin et juillet 2016.

### Page 2

Conjoncture française
54 Note de conjoncture
En novembre 2018, la confiance des ménages
dans la situation économique a nettement
baissé
À côté des principaux indicateurs macroéconomiques,
les enquêtes de conjoncture auprès des entreprises et
des ménages permettent d’avoir une idée de la
manière dont les acteurs économiques envisagent leur
situation à venir.
Mi–décembre, les résultats des enquêtes de
conjoncture de l’Insee auprès des entreprises ne sont

pas encore disponibles pour la période postérieure aux
manifestations émaillées de violence. Le mouvement
des gilets jaunes ayant commencé le 17 novembre, les
chefs d’entreprise n’ont pas pu les prendre en compte
dans leur réponse à l’enquête de novembre.
L ’enquête de conjoncture auprès des ménages affiche
quant à elle une nette dégradation de l’indicateur
synthétique de confiance en novembre. Toutes les
composantes de la confiance sont en baisse marquée.

En particulier, le solde sur l’opportunité de faire des
achats importants chute en novembre ; or ce solde
peut être relié à la consommation future des ménages
(cf. éclairage : Que disent les ménages sur leur
perception des évolutions de leur niveau de vie dans
les enquêtes de conjoncture ?). Il laisse donc présager
une consommation relativement peu dynamique au
quatrième trimestre 2018, trimestre pourtant marqué
par des mesures fiscales (baisse de la taxe d’habitation

et des cotisations salariales) venant augmenter le
pouvoir d’achat des ménages.
Le mouvement social pourrait également
peser sur l’emploi
Enfin, le ralentissement de l’activité lié au mouvement
des gilets jaunes pourrait également affecter la
dynamique des créations d’emploi. Par exemple,
certaines embauches de contrats courts ou d’intérim
prévus dans les magasins à l’occasion des fêtes de fin
d’année pourraient finalement ne pas se concrétiser à

cause des blocages.
Au total, le scénario de cetteNote de conjoncture fait
l’hypothèse que le mouvement des gilets jaunes
pourrait ôter 0,1 point à la croissance du PIB au
quatrième trimestre 2018, via les secteurs d’activité
sans doute principalement touchés : commerce,
hébergement, restauration, transports… Cette
estimation est néanmoins soumise à beaucoup
d’aléas, ne serait–ce que sur la durée du mouvement.
Bibliographie
Insee (2017), « En 2017, le tourisme ne pèserait plus

sur la croissance française, après avoir coûté 0,2
point en 2016 », éclairage de laNote de conjoncture
de juin 2017.
Beatriz (2018), « Le retour des touristes en 2017 a
contribué pour 0,2 point à la croissance française »,
encadré de l’édition 2018 de L’économie française,
comptes et dossiers (Insee). /c110

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至 3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由 2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率 0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因 ，
貢獻整體 CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導 ，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性 ，需求面
通膨略有提高 ，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在 4%以上維持更久 。受
到油價高盤 、通膨僵固 ，與財政惡化 、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響 ，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中 ， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈 ，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大 、
歐元走弱 ，美元轉強 ，商品、黃金承壓 。法
國政治 不確定性讓 歐股修正 、法股最疲 ，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高 ，增加企業借貸成本

與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。

► 美國第三季財報季展開 ，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現 。根據FactSet數據
顯示 ，標普500指數第三季獲利 成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐 ，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI 需求帶動 ， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股 ，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力 。高盛指出 ，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普 500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長 ，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響 ，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間 ，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面 。隨財報
數據陸續公布 ，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現 ，並維持正向財測 ，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價 ，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。

投資焦點
► 醫療保健 、醫療服務 與生物技術等行業 ，在
景氣不好時 ，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高 ，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天 ，股市波動風險上升 ，觀察
過去當 VIX恐慌指數上揚時 ，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯 ，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率 、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑 ，部分公司估值偏低 。當
臨床數據改善 、或導入AI篩選資料與 藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值 。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力 ，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線 ，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值 ，帶動整體產業估值回升 。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場 需求，
創新藥物 (如瘦瘦針 )讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場 2030年銷售額預估上調 15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。

醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健
市場波動上升、醫療保健表現佳，獲利穩定成長

► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響 ，由於商業雇主
贊助保險 (ESI)和平價醫療法案 (ACA)的自付
額增加 ，以及最高自付費用要求 ，較去年同
期提高 ，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數 、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善 。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健 ，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費 ，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限 )來擴大利潤空間 ，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合 ，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險 (ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去 20 年來的最高水準 。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加 ，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑 。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。

股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長 ，市場靜待財報結果與展望 ，和對AI資本支出回收影響 。
考量地緣衝突 、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉 ，短期金融市場紛擾不止 ，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度 。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變 ，逢股市拉回時可分批布局 ，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間 ，殖利率偏高環境使 短中期債券提供吸
引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金

融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高 ，使金價短期承壓 ，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化 ，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Document 349: Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

13030420     1

Transformation 
Quantum dictionary, part 1: Decoding the fundamentals 
21 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• What is quantum computing? How does it work? What is a qubit? And when will quantum reach its full potential? Quantum 
computing challenges our intuition and our understanding of what computers can do. In this two-part dictionary series, we 
decode key terms behind what could be the next major technological revolution. 
• Qubits are the basic building blocks of quantum computers, harnessing properties such as superposition and entanglement to 
process information. However, qubits are highly sensitive to their surroundings where disturbances can lead to errors in 
quantum computation, making error correction a key challenge for building quantum computers. 
• There is no single approach to building a quantum computer. Researchers and companies are pursuing a range of qubit 
technologies, from neutral atoms and photons to spin, superconducting, topological and trapped-ion qubits, while quantum 
computing itself is likely to increasingly work alongside classical computing and AI.

First, let’s lay the groundwork… 
Quantum computing  
Quantum computing uses qubits and quantum properties such as superposition (defined later on) to perform complex 
calculations and solve certain problems faster than classical computers.1 From a practical perspective, quantum computing could 
be used to tackle problems related to optimization, machine learning, simulation and factorization across a range of sectors, 
including autos and logistics, financial services, energy and climate, health and life sciences and national security. 
Current quantum computers can perform ‘just’ thousands of quantum operations. As the technology advances from thousands to 
millions, billions and eventually trillions of operations, quantum computers could begin to have commercial applications.  
• Millions: Financial services and security could experience some of the first use cases, such as threat detection, fraud 
prevention, derivatives pricing, bond trading and arbitrage. 
• Billions: Quantum could help power AI through advanced data generation capabilities.  
• Trillions: Quantum chemistry applications could emerge, including drug discovery, battery simulation and materials 
discovery. 
These are the publicly known applications, according to Oxford Quantum Circuits (OQC). And as quantum computing capabilities 
improve, some of these use cases could emerge sooner than anticipated.  
Now, we’ll tackle the core concepts… 
Qubits 
Qubits (quantum bits) are a quantum computer’s basic unit of information. They can exhibit quantum properties such as 
superposition and entanglement (which we elaborate on below) — properties fundamental to quantum computing. 
Quantum gates manipulate the state of qubits, much like logic gates manipulate bits in classical computers. Despite the name, a 
quantum gate is not a physical object, but rather an operation applied to a quantum system that changes its state. 
From physical to logical qubits 
Physical qubits are the foundation of a quantum computer’s hardware. However, they are highly susceptible to error. To address 
this, multiple physical qubits can be structured together to encode a logical qubit, allowing errors to be detected and corrected

1 Schneider, J., & Smalley, I. (2025, June 10). What is quantum computing? IBM. 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

and making the stored quantum information more reliable. In recent years, according to BofA Global Research, logical qubits 
have become a greater focus for quantum computing companies. 
Entanglement 
Entanglement is a condition where two or more qubits become linked so that their states are correlated, regardless of distance 
(Exhibit 1). It allows quantum computers to process information in ways that can be difficult or impossible for classical 
computers. 
So, how does it work? Suppose we have two photons that have opposite polarizations (the orientation of their light waves). 
Before measurement, neither has a definite polarization. When one is measured as horizontally polarized, the other will be 
vertically polarized when measured in the same basis, wherever it is. When measured separately, they appear to be random, but 
when compared, they are correlated (Exhibit 2). 
The challenges 
Once created, entanglement must be protected from decoherence (where interactions with the environment destroy quantum 
correlations). Maintaining entanglement over long distances is another challenge. Photons traveling through optical fiber can be 
absorbed or scattered, making long-distance quantum communication difficult. 2 Quantum repeaters aim to overcome this 
challenge by extending entanglement across a series of shorter links. 
Exhibit 1: Entanglement is a state where at least two qubits become 
correlated 
An infographic of entangled qubits

Source: BofA Global Research
